#2864. BR 26 club could be mathematically relegated on Tuesday; here's how 🧮

Chapecoense take to the field this Tuesday (3) facing the possibility of having their relegation from the Brazilian Championship mathematically confirmed.

The Santa Catarina side, who sit bottom of the table with 21 points, host Mirassol at Arena Condá at 7:30 p.m. in a match in the 34th round.

Their opponents from São Paulo state are the first team outside the relegation zone, with 37 points.

If they lose today, the green-and-white side will officially be relegated to Série B.

A defeat would leave them 16 points behind Mirassol. With Chapecoense having only five regular fixtures remaining (15 points available) and a game in hand against Vasco, the gap would become mathematically insurmountable.

A draw could also seal relegation, though confirmation would have to wait until the following day: the club would move to 22 points, leaving them 15 behind Grêmio, and would go down if the Rio Grande do Sul side pick up any points in their fixture this round.

Their top-flight campaign has seen them record the competition's weakest attack (32 goals scored) and leakiest defense (66 goals conceded), with just four wins, nine draws and 19 defeats.

The impending relegation comes the season after the club's promotion, having gone up last year after finishing third in Série B with 62 points, behind only Coritiba and Athletico.

The current relegation battle involves teams from 12th-placed Botafogo (39 points) down, followed by Vasco (13th, 38 points), Vitória (14th, 38 points), Grêmio (15th, 37 points), Mirassol (16th, 37 points), as well as the clubs currently in the bottom four.

In 17th place with 36 points, Corinthians occupy the first relegation spot in the 2026 Brazilian Championship, followed by Internacional (18th, 35 points), Remo (19th, 29 points) and bottom-placed Chape (21 points).

Dončić's 21 points in the opening quarter of the Lakers' Friday road game against the Warriors was his 33rd career 20-point quarter and 10th 20-point quarter as a Laker.

He joined Bryant as the only two Lakers players to record at least 10 20-point quarters since 2000.

Bryant recorded 36 20-point quarters between 2000 and his retirement in 2016.

With Doncic entering Monday averaging a league-best 37.7 points per game, the highest scoring average in league history by a non-Wilt Chamberlain player, he's bound to make more history this season.

#2862. Midterm elections could jolt stocks if Democrats sweep Congress

The stock market is not yet pricing in a Democratic sweep of Congress — despite some polls and most prediction markets pointing that way — so that scenario could weigh on share prices Wednesday, according to investors and analysts.

Republicans managing to keep control could cause a short-term relief rally, they said.

Short-term moves aside, the typical post-midterm pattern in the months ahead is bullish. But some wonder if the market could buck the positive historical trend this time because the S&P 500 is already up so much this year going into the election.

As far as certain stocks to watch, a big Democratic win on Tuesday night — especially in the Texas governor race — could add fuel to an AI stock sell-off on the notion that more data center restrictions would gain momentum nationally. Defense stocks could come under pressure on fears of limits on spending.

Polls are pointing to a Democratic victory in the House and a toss-up in the Senate. Prediction markets have been more favorable to Democrats, calling for a blue sweep.

While investors are signaling "begrudging acceptance" to a base case scenario that there will indeed be a Democratic sweep, that's not how they're positioning, said Tobin Marcus, head of U.S. policy and politics at Wolfe Research. The firm's recent survey data among more than 100 institutional investors suggests they are not strongly positioned for any particular result.

"Investors seem to have a significant dose of skepticism about whether the final outcome is going to be quite as favorable to Democrats as the polling suggests," Marcus told CNBC, noting that the firm shares the view that the "polling is likely overstating Democratic strength."

JPMorgan sees a blue wave knocking the S&P 500 by about a half percent on Wednesday.

On the other hand, if Republicans end up retaining both chambers of Congress, that could spur a rally, said Sameer Samana, head of global equities and real assets at Wells Fargo Investment Institute.

"From a certainty standpoint, the market knows what to do with Republicans holding both chambers," he said.

Citigroup strategists said Monday that Republicans losing the House but managing to keep the Senate could be "a tailwind for equities into year-end." JPMorgan traders predict up to a 1% pop on Wednesday if Republicans hold on to that chamber.

Looking past any short-term move, stocks tend to rally after the midterms have concluded. In fact, the S&P 500 was up one year out after all midterm elections going back to the early 1980s.

But the S&P 500 has already recorded double-digit gains so far in 2026, so some on Wall Street worry that investors are being overly complacent heading into this year's elections.

During midterm election years, the S&P 500 has typically averaged a 14% drawdown at some point in the second half of the year found Jonathan Krinsky, chief market technician at BTIG.

"The reason stocks often perform well in November during midterms is because [the S&P 500] typically is weak INTO the elections. We have the opposite setup this year," Krinsky wrote last week. "Therefore, while consensus is that we see a post midterm rally, we think the opposite is actually more likely."

It's not helping that the broader market has recently come under significant pressure due to turbulence in the AI trade, which led the Nasdaq Composite to post its worst day since June on Friday. AI shares led the market lower again on Monday.

A strong showing from Democrats in Tuesday's midterm elections could pressure the trade even more.

At the state level, increased Democratic control is probably a headwind for stocks associated with data centers and technological capital equipment, analysts said.

"It [would solidify] the early kind of cautionary signs in different states around the advancement of AI infrastructure," Rob Rains at Washington Analysis told CNBC.

Goldman Sachs strategists said this week that a Democratic Congress "might limit data center tax incentives, but a national moratorium looks unlikely in any election outcome."

One example would be Texas — a key hub of the data-center buildout. A surprise loss there by Republican Gov. Greg Abbott has the "potential to spook" the market in the short term, Wolfe's Marcus said.

A blue wave might pressure defense shares, while boosting health care stocks, analysts and investors said.

"A substantial Democratic sweep could limit real spending growth unless a broader agreement increases both defense and health-care funding," said Alex Sagal, global equity analyst at Wells Fargo Investment Institute. However, he noted that the earnings outlook for the defense industry looks "stable to modestly positive."

Wolfe Research's Marcus said that healthcare is going to be "the biggest focus," as Democrats will push for restoring expired Affordable Care Act enhanced premium subsidies or unwinding some of the Medicaid reforms in the One Big Beautiful Bill Act.

"Healthcare stocks have been doing really well lately," Jed Ellerbroek, portfolio manager at Argent Capital Management, said to CNBC. "I think part of that is the anti-AI trade, but I also think part of it is just optimism that Democrats are taking over."

Ellerbroek is keeping his eye on hospital-related stocks such as HCA Healthcare and Tenet Healthcare as well as health insurers like UnitedHealth and Centene.

Financials was the worst S&P 500 sector in early 2026 on the heels of President Donald Trump calling on Congress to pass a law that would cap credit card interest rates at 10%. While the sector has been able to notch gains since, with its year-to-date rise now at around 3%, things could get more unstable for the group under Democratic control.

"Democrats will come in wanting to show tangible progress on the affordability issue, and that means targeting sectors that are front and center in consumer budgets," said Evercore ISI's Matthew Aks. "We know Trump has populist instincts, populist leanings, and could he pick a couple of issues and decide to team up with the Democrats on those issues and target those industries in a populist way? Absolutely."

Given that Democratic control is often associated with increased regulation, while Republican control is thought to promote deregulation, Democrats could be interested in passing credit card rate caps if they come out victorious.

"I think if Democrats have control of one or both chambers, they would try to pass it and see if they could get Trump to either sign it or force him to veto it, and that would let Democrats kind of score a political point," Aks said.

Trump could restart the war against Iran after holding off any major strikes leading up to the midterms, a move that could upend the overall market and cause energy stocks to outperform. Crude oil prices jumped more than 10% in October, though they gave a lot of that gain back on Monday.

Gasoline prices stood at $4.17 a gallon on average nationwide Sunday, a nearly 40% increase compared to the same period last year, per AAA.

"If there are escalations, that could obviously put upward pressure on prices just ahead of Thanksgiving," said Patrick De Haan, head of petroleum analysts at Gas Buddy, "which is also when Americans have really staunch traditions in terms of driving to friends and family."

If Trump decides to take forceful action, it could snuff out hope for a typical post-midterm year-end rally.

Evercore ISI said the S&P 500 could retreat by double-digits to end the year at 6,800 as oil spikes if the president "moves to kinetic escalation in Iran ahead of seating the new Congress in January" and Democrats take Congress.

— With reporting by Sarah Min, Tobias Burns and Spencer Kimball

#2861. AFC North Standings: Bengals survive Titans scare; Steelers take first place

The Cincinnati Bengals survived a wild 43-36 shootout with the Tennessee Titans behind six total touchdowns from Joe Burrow, including a nine-yard scramble for the winning score in overtime. The results provided plenty of drama across the division, but perhaps the biggest takeaway was the Bengals' defense, which looked disturbingly similar to the unit that plagued Cincinnati throughout last season.

The Cincinnati Bengals escaped with a 43-36 overtime victory over the Tennessee Titans on Sunday, but the final score did little to disguise another troubling defensive performance. Joe Burrow threw five touchdown passes and had three interceptions in a game that featured dramatic momentum swings, costly mistakes, and enough defensive breakdowns to remind Bengals fans of last season's struggles. Cincinnati ultimately prevailed with an offensive touchdown in overtime for just the second time in franchise history.

Tennessee wasted little time exposing Cincinnati's defensive problems, jumping to a 13-0 first-quarter lead. Tony Pollard broke loose for a 40-yard touchdown run, and the Titans repeatedly found opportunities against a Bengals defense that struggled to contain the run and prevent sustained drives. Cincinnati eventually responded behind Burrow, who connected with Ja'Marr Chase for a 30-yard touchdown on third-and-18 and found rookie tight end Jack Endries for another score before halftime. The Bengals took a 14-13 lead into the locker room despite an uneven opening half.

Burrow's afternoon was a study in extremes. His five touchdown passes demonstrated the explosive potential of Cincinnati's offense, but three interceptions by Tennessee safety Amani Hooker repeatedly threatened to undo that production. Cincinnati benefited from a defensive takeaway of its own when Boye Mafe forced a Cam Ward fumble that Dexter Lawrence recovered, setting up Burrow's 17-yard touchdown pass to Tee Higgins.

Yet the biggest concern was the Bengals' defense. Despite offseason efforts to improve the unit, Cincinnati surrendered 36 points in regulation to a Tennessee team that entered the game with just one victory. Pollard's running and Ward's ability to move the offense repeatedly put the Bengals on their heels. The performance was an uncomfortable reminder of last season, when defensive shortcomings regularly forced Burrow and the offense to compensate for mistakes on the other side of the ball.

The Pittsburgh Steelers left little doubt about who belongs atop the AFC North on Sunday, overwhelming the Cleveland Browns 27-6. Pittsburgh (6-2) won its fourth consecutive game, avenging an earlier loss to Cleveland and claiming sole possession of first place in the division. The Browns fell to 5-3 after failing to score a touchdown against a relentless Steelers defense.

Pittsburgh controlled the game on the ground, with Jaylen Warren rushing for 143 yards on 17 carries and Rico Dowdle adding 56 yards and a touchdown. The Steelers piled up 160 rushing yards in the first half alone, building a 20-6 halftime advantage. Tight end Pat Freiermuth scored on a one-yard sneak with one second remaining in the second quarter, capping a late drive that effectively put Cleveland on its heels.

The Steelers' defense was equally impressive, recording eight sacks, forcing two turnovers and limiting Cleveland to just 34 rushing yards. T.J. Watt collected two sacks, while Patrick Queen contributed 10 tackles, 1.5 sacks and a forced fumble. Cleveland managed minus one yard of offense in the opening quarter, and quarterback Deshaun Watson spent much of the afternoon on his back.

Aaron Rodgers completed 13 of 25 passes for 172 yards, including a 58-yard connection to Darnell Washington that set up Warren's third-quarter touchdown and pushed Pittsburgh's lead to 27-6. Washington finished with a career-best 94 receiving yards as the Steelers cruised to their 23rd consecutive regular-season home victory over the Browns.

The Baltimore Ravens suffered a disastrous afternoon in Buffalo on Sunday, falling 36-10 to the Bills in a game that quickly became a nightmare for their quarterback room. Lamar Jackson, who had missed the previous three games with an ankle injury, hurt his thumb on Baltimore's first offensive play and never returned. Backup Tyler Huntley also suffered an injury, forcing the Ravens to turn to third-string quarterback Joe Fagnano. The loss dropped Baltimore to 4-4 and extended its losing streak to three games.

Buffalo jumped ahead 10-0 on Josh Allen's one-yard touchdown run and a 56-yard field goal by Tyler Bass. Derrick Henry briefly brought Baltimore back into contention with a two-yard touchdown run, but the Ravens struggled to generate offense with their depleted quarterback group. Buffalo led 16-7 at halftime, and Baltimore managed only a third-quarter field goal before the Bills took complete control. James Cook scored on a five-yard run, while Allen connected with DJ Moore for an 11-yard touchdown and Dalton Kincaid for a 23-yard score.

Allen's touchdown pass to Moore was particularly significant, moving him past Jim Kelly for the most career touchdown passes in Bills franchise history. Buffalo improved to 6-1, while Baltimore faces mounting questions about its offense and the health of its quarterbacks.

However, the 27-year-old is not prepared to make a decision without first understanding his role under Hansi Flick.

Bastoni has rejected Inter Milan's contract proposal (Photo by Angel Martinez/Getty Images)

#2858. Which NFC contenders will miss the playoffs?

With the Chicago Bears' emphatic win over the Seattle Seahawks on Monday night, the NFC playoff picture is starting to become more clear, and it sure involves a lot of NFC North teams. After eight weeks, here's the full playoff picture in the NFC.

5. Bears: 6-26. Lions: 5-27. Packers: 5-3

8. Seahawks: 4-49. Rams: 4-410. Buccaneers: 3-511. Falcons: 3-512. Cardinals: 3-513. Cowboys: 3-514. Commanders: 2-4-115. Saints: 2-516. Eagles: 2-6

The way I currently see it, the only teams truly in contention are the top nine teams listed in the standings. Granted, the Bucs and Falcons could very well jump the Panthers in the NFC South, but it feels like a pretty safe bet at this point that only one of those three teams will make the postseason. Combine that with a Giants team comfortably in the lead in the NFC East, and there are really only five other playoff spots that seem up for grabs and seven teams vying for those five spots:

All seven of those teams have seen varying levels of success, and each team has some very strong strengths, and some weak weaknesses.

My answer: First, I just want to point to how difficult this question is. I think all seven of these teams are likely worthy of the postseason, and it's unfortunate we have to let an NFC South team take at least one of their places.

Let me go backwards and announce the teams I'm most comfortable in making the playoffs. I think the Bears and 49ers both have a strong case for best team in the conference, as they are the only teams in the NFC that have both a top-10 scoring offense and defense.

Interestingly, the Rams are the next closest team with the 10th and 12th ranked offense/defense, so I'm going to have them IN, as well, despite their slow start. They've got a couple of cupcake games (Commanders, Cardinals) before their bye, and they should be much healthier by then. It's admittedly a gauntlet of games after that (Packers, Chiefs, 49ers, Seahawks x2), but I've got them in.

I'm not 100% confident in the Lions, but I'm going to have them in, too. Their offense is humming with back-to-back strong performances against two of the best defenses in football (despite the low scoring). However, I am still significantly worried about their defense, particularly with the recent injuries. The defense played well over the last three games, but those were three poor offenses (Cardinals, Packers, Vikings).

That leaves the Vikings, Packers, and Seahawks fighting for the final spot. Before I dive into them, it's worth considering strength of remaining schedule. The Vikings have by far the hardest road of these three teams.

Remaining strength of schedule, by opponent's win percentage:

However, Minnesota has a two game lead over Green Bay and three over Seattle.

Could the Vikings truly fall out of playoff contention after a 6-0 start? The next three games (vs. Bills, at Packers, at 49ers) will tell us a lot. But they also have games against the Falcons, Panthers, Commanders, and Jets remaining. I think they likely get to 10-11 wins, which will probably be enough to make it.

The Packers and their struggling run game will also be tested soon. Their next three games are Patriots, Vikings, and Rams—with the Bills, Bears, and Lions still on the docket. They lose all of those games, and they're already at nine losses.

And while the Seahawks have the easiest schedule, with games against the Raiders, Cardinals, Cowboys, Eagles, and Panthers remaining, I can see them dropping games to the 49ers, Giants, and Rams (x2) still. That offense is looking putrid right now, and I'm not sure how much their admittedly elite defense can help.

So my two teams out are the Packers and Seahawks. However, I think every NFC team outside the Bears and 49ers are capable of falling out of the race.

Which two teams do you think will fall out of the race? Answer in the comment section below.

Walker Kessler had six points for Los Angeles.

Los Angeles subbed in its bench with 1:22 left, and they kept the lead this time without making it unnecessarily interesting.

Luka finished with 42 points, seven rebounds and 12 assists. Reaves ended with 27 points and five assists while shooting 10-15 from the field. Kessler notched 15 points with 15 rebounds.

In his return from injury, Williams poured in nine points off the bench. Mamu pitched in with 13 points and four assists.

The rest of the role players all had positive impacts in small ways across the board. Thybulle scored six points with two assists, Grimes had seven points with four assists, Collin Sexton logged six points and LaRavia finished with seven points, four rebounds, two assists and two blocks.

The Lakers' next game will be against the Sacramento Kings on Wednesday at 7:00 PM PT.

#2852. Shemar Stewart’s time to show the Bengals what he’s made of has come

"While he has the athletic measurements of a No. 1 overall pick, his production left a whole lot to be desired."

"The lack of production relative to the traits is a concern; still, players who move like him are highly coveted."

"He's also the highest-rated player in this year's balancing act of marrying future NFL potential with on-field production. And his evaluation might be defined by the number "4.5″ — as in, 4.5 total college sacks and exactly 1.5 in each of his three seasons."

Those are three quotes from three different draft analysts regarding Cincinnati Bengals edge rusher and 2025 first-round pick Shemar Stewart's bizarre lack of production in college despite his borderline freakish combination of size and speed.

The Bengals, of course, used the No. 17 overall selection on Stewart. To this point in his career, he has two sacks to his name and has played in 12 total games.

Most damning, though, was the fact that he was a healthy scratch before the Bengals' Week 7 win over the Ravens on the road in Baltimore, meaning he was fully healthy and available to the Bengals, and the coaching staff felt they had a better chance to win without him. Of course, he wasn't the only player to be a healthy scratch, but he was the only first-round pick.

Stewart was active in Week 8, but only took six snaps.

It appears as though Stewart's time to prove he was worth the No. 17 overall pick has come.

Promising edge rusher and fellow Texas A&M Aggie Cashius Howell suffered a wrist injury that required surgery during their comeback win over the Ravens. The rookie, who has taken 185 defensive snaps before he left in the third quarter against the Titans, which is the third most of any edge rusher on the roster behind Boye Mafe and Myles Murphy, leaves a large void.

Now, Stewart will be asked to help fill that empty space, which will finally give him the opportunity to prove the Bengals got it right in 2025.

This is especially true considering Cedric Johnson (who has taken 104 snaps) also left the game against the Ravens. He was declared out after suffering an ankle injury in the fourth quarter. He is considered day-to-day, but that doesn't necessarily mean he'll be available or at 100% if he is on Sunday morning in Spain.

Stewart's size and speed should make him a nightmare for many offensive tackles. The potential Stewart could have as a pass rusher is why he was selected so early despite the little production. Hopefully, he can help the Bengals remain near the top when it comes to sacks in the league, even though they rarely blitz, which is a testament to their defensive line that Stewart will now look to boost.

#2851. The midterm election could reshape the muni bond market. Here’s what investors need to know

The outcome of Tuesday's election is expected to have broad implications across the market, including municipal bonds.

The spotlight has been on the battle for congressional control and whether Democrats will wrest the House of Representatives, the Senate or both from Republicans. But for the muni market, what happens at the state level could have bigger repercussions — at least in the near term.

There is an array of initiatives on ballots around the nation that could change tax laws and add to bond supply. Taxes matter to muni investors since the income received is free of federal tax, as well as state tax if the holder lives in the state where the bond is issued.

Depending on the location, ballot measures are up for approval that would either provide tax breaks, or raise taxes on the wealthy.

"It's a complete bifurcation between red and blue states," said Dan Close, head of municipals at Nuveen. "Blue states are trying to put in place graduated income tax structures, trying to capture more revenue from high-income earners. Red states are promoting affordability by trying to cut property taxes."

Red states are those that predominantly vote Republican, while blue states swing toward Democrats.

For instance, California has a proposition known as the "billionaire tax," which would impose a one-time levy equal to 5% of the wealth of certain taxpayers with at least $1 billion in assets. The state also has a measure on the ballot that would make a temporary tax hike permanent on individuals earning over $371,000, adjusted annually for inflation. The tax is currently set to expire in 2031.

"The impact to the muni bond market will be to make the exemption more valuable," Close said of the possible passage of tax increases. Investors will likely seek shelter in the muni market, which would send bond prices higher, he noted.

It is a different story in Florida, where voters are set to decide whether to increase the Sunshine State's homestead exemption for non-school district property taxes. Qualified homeowners currently have a $25,000 property tax exemption, as well as another for $25,000 that doesn't apply to school property taxes. The new proposal raises the latter exemption to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 by 2028. New residents have a five-year waiting period before receiving the benefit.

If passed, some local governments will have to find other revenue sources or increase the overall tax rate to replace what they've lost, said Tom Kozlik, head of public policy and municipal strategy at HilltopSecurities.

"It's not necessarily going to result in downgrades for sure, but there would have to be some balancing with regard to overall revenues," he said.

Not all municipalities will be hurt equally, which means credit research is important, said Close.

"Like everything in municipal finance, it is really on a case-by-case basis that you could have some credit deterioration from [a] rollback of property taxes," he said.

Other voters on Tuesday will be asked to approve more state or local government debt. That could weigh on a market that has already seen heightened supply.

Some $473.8 billion has already been issued year to date, through September, according to the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association. That's a 7.8% increase over the same period a year ago. In 2025, total muni issuance reached $587.3 billion, 14.3% higher than 2024, Sifma said.

Cooper Howard, director of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research, believes supply is the biggest issue coming out of the election.

"That amount of issuance has been one of the reasons why you have to see that strong amount of demand continue to keep pace with it for total returns to keep up," Howard said.

Fortunately, new supply doesn't necessarily come quickly into the market. "There's a window over a couple years that they can issue," he noted.

The ultimate makeup of the next Congress could also have consequences for muni investors as lawmakers sort out their priorities. Most notable is what lawmakers will do to tackle the country's affordability problem, Kozlik at Hilltop said.

"There could be some tax policy initiatives and even affordability-related initiatives that manifest themselves in the first quarter of next year," he said. "That could impact housing. It could impact higher education. It could impact public power financing."

Investors considering moves before the election may want to look at those states with tax increases on the ballot, said Close at Nuveen.

For instance, Nuveen recently began offering a separately managed account dedicated to Washington after getting an enormous number of inquiries from financial advisors. The state recently enacted a 9.9% tax on individuals with taxable annual income above $1 million, set to go into effect in 2028. But the issue is now being put to voters, who can decide whether to repeal the new law.

"At the margin, if you do think that some of these measures are going to pass, knowing that bonds usually move up with an increase in taxes, there might be some opportunities to buy ahead of the election for blue states," Close said.

Howard believes munis are attractive right now, particularly for those who are in a tax bracket of 24% or above. Fundamentals are good and the bonds largely have good credit quality, he said. "About 70% of the broad muni index is either triple-A or double-A rated."

Kozlik is also bullish, calling yields "really attractive." He suggests investors concentrate on AA-rated or higher bonds.

"The muni space is part of the portfolio that most investors want," he said. "It helps them sleep well at night."

#2846. Player Grades: The Spurs Run the Jazz off the River Walk in San Antonio

After pushing the San Antonio Spurs to overtime last Wednesday, the Utah Jazz tried to make a similar stand in Alamo City on Monday night. But the home team, spurred on by a raucous crowd, had other ideas, outmuscling the visitors 119-97. It was Utah's second blow out loss in a row, leaving fans (and this fan in particular) scrambling to find a silver lining. I won't say the dark clouds are lifting just yet, but there are a few glints of sun filtering through.

Lauri Markkanen A-: After a disappointing performance against Portland on Halloween, Lauri brushed off some of the cobwebs, finding his range from three and also setting up his teammates, delivering perfect lobs for both Jaxson Hayes and Mo Bamba. He was active around the basket, raking in a team high 8 rebounds, and led Utah's spirited (but short lived) come back effort in the second quarter.

Josh Okogie A: Josh was excellent tonight, forcing turnovers and pressuring the Spurs' guards into giving up the ball. He also showed some of his offensive skills, getting to the free throw line, where he was 6 of 6. The Jazz just have a different intensity when Okogie is on the floor, something they desperately needed on a tough shooting night.

Mo Bamba A: Another solid night from Mo, scoring 8 points, grabbing 8 rebounds and blocking two shots in just 18 minutes. He is finding ways to impact opponents' shots without fouling, not an easy task when facing a physical team like the Spurs.

Ace Bailey C: A difficult night for Ace, who missed his first six shots from the floor. The Jazz could have really used his scoring, especially in the first and third quarters. On a positive note, he did find some other ways to impact the game, picking up 6 rebounds, 2 assists and a steal.

Keyonte George C: I think Keyonte has spoiled us with his consistent play to start the season. Tonight he struggled against the length and aggressiveness of the Spurs' guards. He was only 25% from the field and 20% from three, and had no assists. He was active on the boards, pulling in 7 rebounds.

Darryn Peterson B: The rookie led the team with 6 assists and also showed some defensive grit with 3 steals and 3 blocks. He took care of the ball, which was in his hands a lot, committing only one turnover. His shooting is still a little streaky—he shot 0-5 in the first half—but once that becomes more consistent, then the sky is the limit.

Josh Green C: There is no denying the effort, but Josh's impact on the game was minimal, hitting one three and grabbing one rebound in 15 minutes on the court.

Brice Sensabaugh D: Brice's most notable play was a flagrant foul, pushing Harrison Barnes to the ground after catching an elbow in the nose. It showed some of the fight that I'm sure Coach Hardy would like to see more of. But Brice's shooting at the Frost Bank Center was mainly ice cold, going 20% from the field and 1-6 from three.

Svi Mykhailiuk D: Svi, normally ready when called on, also had an off night, going 1-7 from the field and 1-4 from three.

Isaiah Collier B: We didn't see Isaiah until the fourth quarter when the game was out of reach. It would have been nice to see his speed and physicality earlier, as it brought an energy to the Jazz's offense (albeit against largely bench players).

Kyle Filipowski C: Kyle has largely been the forgotten man this season, pushed further down the bench by the emergence of the two Joshes. He came in tonight for the last 8 minutes, getting 1 point from the free throw line and grabbing 2 rebounds.

Tony Bradley B: Tony also came in for the last few minutes of the game and was active, muscling in a bucket and grabbing 3 rebounds and a steal.

#2845. NFL power rankings Week 9: Where do Bears, Seahawks settle after Monday night clash?

NFL power rankings entering Week 9 of the 2026 season (previous rank in parentheses):

1. San Francisco 49ers (1): They come out of the bye week with needed rest for their battered stars, eyeing a schedule that only requires them to play on the road once in November, all while welcoming DT DeForest Buckner back into the building.

2. Chicago Bears (2): QB Caleb Williams and newly acquired DT Jeffery Simmons certainly sowed fresh hope in front of a national audience on Monday night that Chicago might get its first NFL title in four decades after Da new-look Bears knocked off the reigning Super Bowl champion Seahawks. In Seattle. In prime time. Quite a statement win.

3. Denver Broncos (3): If they don't have your respect yet, then how about this nugget – they've won five consecutive home games against former league MVPs. This 6-2 squad has also survived the most brutal stretch of its schedule and is set up to coast to another AFC West crown if it can take care of business.

4. Jacksonville Jaguars (7): As their AFC South brethren break up their rosters, the Jags should be on autopilot to a second straight division title. Doesn't mean they don't have issues after losing two defensive starters, S Eric Murray and DT DaVon Hamilton, for the season Sunday.

5. New England Patriots (8): They've only scored more than 30 points once this season as they navigate their offensive injuries. But the Pats can still play mean defense, giving up more than 30 points only once this season.

6. Buffalo Bills (9): They've only failed to score more than 30 points twice this season as rookie HC Joe Brady and QB Josh Allen continue dialing up the hits. But the Bills defense still needs to catch up, giving up at least 30 points four times.

7. Detroit Lions (11): Does there need to be concern about RB Jahmyr Gibbs' usage at some point? He's on pace for 464 touches, which would be the second-highest total in NFL history. (James Wilder had 492 in 1984.)

8. Minnesota Vikings (5): Live by the blitzing broadsword, die by the blitzing broadsword. However, the bigger question surrounding the Vikes as their 10-game winning streak comes to an end might be whether they have the right guy at quarterback.

9. Seattle Seahawks (4): It's far from time to panic. Let's give LG Quenton Nelson and RBs Jadarian Price and Zach Charbonnet some time to jell in a reconfigured offense – especially now that schedule seems poised to let up.

10. Kansas City Chiefs (6): Of QB Patrick Mahomes' 321 passing yards on Sunday at Denver, 72% went to his running backs and tight ends. Here's hoping K.C. can find him a difference-making wideout ahead of the Nov. 10 trade deadline.

11. Green Bay Packers (12): If their season wasn't already belatedly taking off, it's about to gain some serious altitude with defensive game wrecker Micah Parsons set to rejoin practice this week.

12. Los Angeles Rams (13): If their season wasn't already belatedly taking off, it's about to gain some serious altitude with defensive game wrecker Myles Garrett set to rejoin practice this week.

13. Pittsburgh Steelers (16): The AFC North leaders have a league-high 26 sacks, yet rank 26th in the league with a 32.6% pressure rate. Go figure.

14. Cincinnati Bengals (17): A 5-2 start is their best since 2021, one that led them to Super Bowl 56. But Sunday's 43-36 overtime defeat of the Titans sure evoked troubling memories of the post-2021 Bengals.

15. Las Vegas Raiders (14): RB Ashton Jeanty has rushed for fewer than 50 yards in more than half of his 25 NFL starts. Not great.

16. Cleveland Browns (15): DE Jared Verse got taken down Sunday, but he's still searching for his first sack eight games into his Browns career.

17. Tampa Bay Buccaneers (18): With two INTs for 20 yards, LB Alex Anzalone was the Bucs' leading receiver Sunday – which is perfectly fine for a team that's rushed for more than 1,000 yards over its past five outings.

18. Atlanta Falcons (10): QB Michael Penix Jr. and Co. looked every bit a dome team during Sunday's loss in a Tampa monsoon. Certainly an incentive to nail down the home playoff game an NFC South title would confer.

19. New Orleans Saints (20): Six NFL rushers league-wide eclipsed 140 yards Sunday. The Saints only have one player who's gained more than 140 yards on the ground ... this season − RB Kendre Miller's 159 barely eclipsing that figure.

20. New York Giants (22): So a team that allowed RB Saquon Barkley to leave in free agency is in hot pursuit of Indianapolis Colts RB Jonathan Taylor? Weird.

21. Washington Commanders (25): Despite their multitudinous mistakes this season, no team has turned the ball over fewer times (3) − which is exactly why the Commanders (still) have a legit shot to win the NFC East.

22. Carolina Panthers (19): What to do when the offense is averaging 16 points and 262 yards during a discouraging two-game tailspin? Fire the defensive coordinator. Naturally.

23. Arizona Cardinals (26): "If you guys have ever seen 'Napoleon Dynamite,' I've always known (Gardner Minshew) could throw a pigskin a quarter mile," DL L.J. Collier said after Sunday's upset of the Cowboys. "Minshew Magic is real." Nothing further to add here about the Cards' new QB1.

24. Baltimore Ravens (21): Can deploying the wishbone Thursday night against Jacksonville really be a worse option than potentially having to start former UFL "star" Austin Reed, who's never taken a regular-season snap in the NFL, at quarterback?

25. Dallas Cowboys (23): They had been 69-0 at home when nursing a lead of at least 14 points in the fourth quarter … but that was before Sunday.

26. Houston Texans (24): As generally bad as this 2-5 outfit has been, their schedule still very much portends a world where Houston winds up 10-7.

27. Miami Dolphins (27): As generally bad as this 1-6 outfit has been, their schedule still very much portends a world where Miami winds up … 3-14.

28. New York Jets (28): If the season ended today − it doesn't − they'd have three top-10 draft picks in 2027 and a seemingly solid WR3 in Adonai Mitchell as the dust continues to settle from last year's trade deadline fireworks.

29. Tennessee Titans (30): They came out swinging without Simmons, a 31st-ranked defense getting major contributions from the likes of S Amani Hooker (3 INTs of Joe Burrow on Sunday) and DE Jacob Martin (3 sacks of Joe Burrow on Sunday). Throw in QB Cam Ward producing his 300-yard passing game, and maybe there is hope that this team can make strides − even as it resets.

30. Philadelphia Eagles (29): You can bet a team on a six-game slide will look much better Sunday against the division-leading Giants … if only because Philly will be in its Kelly Green throwbacks.

#2839. Luka Dončić shows how unbeatable Lakers can be in rout of Heat

LOS ANGELES — Luka Dončić has had himself a day.

Dončić, over a 24-hour stretch, dropped a staggering 87 points in consecutive Los Angeles Lakers victories, the latest Monday, Nov. 2 in a 138-117 domination over the Miami Heat. In the process, not only did Dončić outshine Heat star Giannis Antetokounmpo, he's proving that Los Angeles is close to unbeatable when he's besieging opponents with buckets.

Case in point: entering the night, NBA teams were averaging 113.7 points per game; the Lakers, by the end of the third quarter, were already at 111. For reference, that was four points off from tying the most allowed by the Heat through three quarters in their history.

One night after he dropped 45 in a win over the Toronto Raptors, Dončić toasted the Heat to the tune of 42 points on 14-of-24 shooting, scooping 7 rebounds and dishing out 13 assists.

It didn't matter who Miami sent at Dončić. Versatile defensive wing Andrew Wiggins started the game on Dončić, but Pelle Larsson and Davion Mitchell each got their turn. Later in the third, after Dončić was creeping toward 40 points, Heat coach Erik Spoelstra dug deep in the bench and deployed defensive-specialist guard Dru Smith. The Heat threw double-teams at Dončić, but that didn't matter, either.

Dončić zipped and lofted passes to open teammates who had been left neglected because of those double-teams. By the end of the third, he had already posted double-digit assists.

This is the bewildering thing about defending Dončić: even when you try to rush him, he stubbornly plays at his pace, parries away the additional defensive attention – almost teasing blitzing defenders and daring them to get close – only to pinpoint passes right into his teammates' shooting pockets.

This was an efficient Dončić. He did not need to strafe the rim with shots. He was under control. He was never flustered. And, at least on Monday night, he made his teammates better.

Antetokounmpo, by comparison, finished with 30 points on 10-of-17 shooting, adding 12 rebounds and 7 assists.

The Lakers improved to 6-2 and Dončić is now averaging 38.3 points per game, which is a full 4 points greater than the next closest player. They wield one of the top 3 offenses in the NBA. And as injuries have plagued some of the top teams in the West, like the Oklahoma City Thunder, there could be an opening for the Lakers to blossom into a legitimate title threat with Dončić as the unquestioned anchor of the team.

#2838. Mike Macdonald: We lost in all three phases

Monday night's game in Seattle featured a pair of 2025 NFC playoff teams who would like to be back in the postseason this year and it ended with the Bears celebrating a 34-17 win over the defending Super Bowl champions.

The Seahawks had made it a three-point game early in the fourth quarter, but a 39-yard screen pass to Kalif Raymond converted a key Bears third down on the next drive and the roof caved in on the Seahawks from there. The Bears ended the night with 111 more offensive yards than the Seahawks and their defense thwarted Seattle on 10 of their 11 third down attempts over the course of the night, which led Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald to say after the game that "there's no other way to sugarcoat" the way his team got beat.

Seahawks kicker Jason Myers missed an extra point and had a field goal blocked and the Seahawks committed several personal fouls to add to their woes on a night when the Bears were clearly better across the board.

"They made it happen, and we didn't," Macdonald said, via the team's website. "It's a team effort; we lost as a team. In all three phases, we can have better plans, we can coach it better, prepare our players better, and we can play better. We'll go back to work and go attack the heck out of it. The margins in our league are too small, and when you don't do the things that it takes to win, that's how a game like that shapes up. It was a team effort. We lost in all three phases."

The Seahawks overwhelmed the Chiefs in a Week 7 win, but Monday night was the kind of step back that explains why they're 4-4 on the season. Having it happen against another NFC contender at home makes the blow more painful and Macdonald said the Seahawks "have some work to do" if they're going to position themselves for another run at the end of this season.

#2836. Yikes! One Bad Hole Cost Doug Ghim $255,250

Golf punishes mistakes with brutal math, and Doug Ghim felt that sting on Monday at the VidantaWorld Mexico Open. He entered the final hole tied for second place, needing only a par to lock in a $454,000 paycheck. Instead, he carded a double bogey that slashed his winnings to $198,750. That single shot on the 18th hole erased $255,250 from his bank account, a massive hit for a player who has earned over $10 million in his career.

The sequence of errors was painful to watch for any fan who understands the stakes. Ghim's tee shot drifted left into the rough on the sharp dogleg right hole, avoiding the water but landing in a bad spot. His approach shot fell short and right, burying itself in lush rough that made contact difficult. He hacked out, missed the green with his fourth shot, and two putts later the damage was done. The result was a T-6 finish that left him with a sour taste in his mouth and a lighter wallet.

This incident highlights how the modern purse structure creates high-risk moments on the final hole. Central Oregon Daily reported that Ghim was finishing the 2026 season with some of his finest golf. A runner-up finish at last month's Bank of Utah Championship was among seven consecutive top-20 results he achieved. His consistent play pushed him to 55th place in the 2027 FedEx Cup Fall standings, securing entry into the opening two signature events.

Andrew Novak, who won the tournament with a five-shot cushion, was safe from this specific financial drama. Novak posted a 25-under par total of 259, setting a new tournament record with rounds of 61 and 62 on Friday and Saturday. Yahoo Sports noted that Novak equaled Austin Smotherman's lowest 36-hole score record at the Bank of Utah Championship for the current season. While Novak celebrated his first solo title, Ghim faced the cold reality of the leaderboard math.

The financial loss is immediate, but the impact on momentum is the real story for a player in Ghim's position. He is still searching for his first PGA Tour win, and this collapse on the 18th hole at Vidanta Vallarta is a heavy psychological burden. The runner-up prize of $534,000 went to Steven Fisk and Kristoffer Ventura, who both finished at 20-under par. Mackenzie Hughes took fourth place with $294,000. Every position above Ghim on the board represents a significant chunk of money he walked away from.

Novak acknowledged the pressure of the season in his post-round comments, noting how difficult the year has been. "This is one I've been working for for a while. Obviously not a great season for how I wanted it to go, but this is a nice way to kind of finish," said Novak. He also expressed satisfaction with his performance, stating, "Just happy with how I played today. I thought I grinded out pretty well." Ghim cannot make similar claims about his grind on that final hole.

Although Ghim will not play in Mexico this week, he will turn his attention right away to the World Wide Technology Championship in Cabo San Lucas, scheduled for next week. Austin, where the former Texas star likely will compete given his current form, is where he may tee it up. He remains in the hunt for the top 51-60 in the final fall standings to secure his signature event starts. That goal is still alive, but the margin for error has shrunk dramatically after Monday's double bogey.

The event offered a $6 million total purse, a slight drop from last year. Even with a smaller purse, the gap between second and sixth place remains wide enough to define a season. Ghim has the talent to win, as his seven straight top-20 finishes prove. Now he must prove he can close out a round without costing himself a quarter-million dollars in a single swing.

#2834. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2831. Player Grades: Recapping the Mavericks’ embarrassing 115-110 loss to the Sacramento Kings

The Dallas Mavericks (4-3) were riding high after beating two previously undefeated teams, but followed that up with a loss to a winless Kings (1-6) team, 115-100. No Kyrie Irving (rest) didn't help the cause, but Cooper Flagg had a rough night, and the Mavericks simply could not connect from downtown as a team.

The silver lining? If the Mavs slept on arguably the worst team in the NBA, they've learned the lesson that you just can't do that in this league.

The good news is that hopefully Flagg got his worst game of the season out of the way. He had a dreadful shooting night (3-of-19) from the floor, didn't have much interest in taking available three-point shots and seemed frustrated to the point it threw his game off entirely. He chipped in little bits elsewhere and bolstered his scoring with excellent free throw shooting, but it wasn't nearly enough with the overall messiness of the game.

You never want to see a player get injured as happened to Christie (hip), but it's the only thing that spared him from a solid "D" grade. He was 0-of-5 – all from downtown – before the injury and sadly has brought very little to the team this season inclusive of Monday night's performance.

Martin wasn't on my starter Bingo card as the guy who would take Kyrie Irving's place in the starting lineup, but keeping the bench unit of Zaccharie Risacher, Morez Johnson and Marcus Sasser made sense. He was fine, but didn't play starter minutes and didn't contribute all that much. So, while he didn't necessarily hurt the team, he didn't quite help it in any appreciable way either.

Washington backslid a little from previous games, appearing a bit disengaged with a stat line to back that up. 2-of-9 shooting (with six misses from deep) and not nearly enough production for the amount of time he spent on the floor made for a bad night. This was a game Washington could have at least locked in on defense, but that is where the disengagement seemed to be apparent. Something was off in Dallas on Monday night, and it definitely got to Washington.

If not for Gafford, it's difficult to imagine how bad this game would have been. He was by far the best player on the floor for the Mavs. An offensive juggernaut hitting 9-of-10 shots and both of his free throw attempts. He made the absolute most of limited minutes and it's a shame it was largely a wasted effort.

Alongside Gafford, Clayton, Jr. was another bright spot. He was the only player on the team even remotely locked in from deep, hitting 4-of-5 three point attempts and 6-of-9 overall. In hindsight, he should have taken Irving's place as a starter, but who could have predicted that from the outset? This was a very nice night for Clayton, Jr. and hopefully something to build off as he will likely have opportunities on night's Irving rests.

This is simply a game you flush. Look around the NBA on any given night, and a team that has no business losing is getting waxed. Tonight it was the Mavs' turn. Almost to a man, they were completely off their game and plainly could not get anything going outside of a few bright spots. Learn from it and get ready for the next one.

I invite you to follow me @_80MPH on X, and check back often at Mavs Moneyball for all the latest on the Dallas Mavericks.

#2830. The Panthers could take a player with a similar story to Efe Obada in the Draft

The 2027 NFL Draft feels like forever away, and we're all caught up in the start of the NFL season. Regardless, it's never too early to look ahead to the Draft. This weekly series will take a closer look at some of the prospects the Carolina Panthers could select in the 2027 iteration of the Draft. In this series, we'll only be looking at prospects the Panthers could seriously consider. This week our profile will focus on Florida Gators defensive lineman Emmanuel Oyebadejo.

Oyebadejo (6'6", 299 lbs.) was a soccer player in Manchester, England growing up. A growth spurt saw him pivot from the pitch to the basketball court, where he played for England's U16 and U18 teams. Oyebadejo originally enrolled at the University of Exeter to study medicine. He started playing American football in 2023 when a coach at Exeter saw the mountain of a man on the sideline and recruited him. His natural affinity for the sport caught the eye of Jacksonville State, who recruited him stateside. After an injury before the 2024 season, he played in all 14 games for the Gamecocks in 2025, getting him some national attention from big time programs. In his first season of college football in the US, he put up 43 tackles, 6.5 tackles for loss, 4.5 sacks, two forced fumbles, and five passes defended. He stayed in state and transferred to Florida, where he already has 4.5 TFLs and 3.5 sacks in just six games so far with the Gators.

Oyebadejo looks like he was built in a lab. He pairs his tall, long, and heavy frame with elite athleticism (4.72 forty at about 300 lbs.). He uses his speed and mass to convert all that momentum into a deadly power rush off the edge but has the versatility to line up anywhere from the B gap to a wide 9 alignment. The level of competition does not seem to bother him too much, notching 2.5 sacks in his first SEC game against Auburn.

Obviously Oyebadejo is a raw prospect since he's only been playing football since 2023. His technique and hand usage need a lot of work, his pad level is often too high, and his tackling is inconsistent. He has also been relegated to a rotational role despite having the raw physical tools to be an every down lineman.

Oyebadejo is a unicorn in terms of height, length, weight, strength, and speed. Even though he has only played in about 20 college games, the upside is incredibly tantalizing. To put his physical attributes into perspective: he is taller, heavier, longer, and faster than JJ Watt was coming out of Wisconsin. That's the type of perfect world scenario teams see in Oyebadejo. Still, right now he's projected as a Day Two pick because the sample size is just so small. He'd maintain a rotational role at first in the NFL, but that is exactly what the Panthers will be looking for next season. With Jaelan Phillips and the returning Nic Scourton projected to be starting on the edge and Derrick Brown, Lee Hunter, and potentially Turk Wharton (assuming he is not a cap casualty) being your starters on the line, Oyebadejo could be a player to slot in for any of those spots aside from nose tackle. That would allow him to slowly acclimate to the NFL game while learning from some very talented pros in the process. The Panthers also have a history of rostering late-to-football guys who grew up in England; Efe Obada was a great story and fan favorite during his time with the team.

What do you think, Panthers fans? If Emmanuel Oyebadejo is available when the Panthers select in the 2027 NFL Draft, should they take him? Sound off in the comments!

#2829. Fantasy Football Week 9 Waiver Wire: 5 Best Pickups

Only two teams will be on a bye during Week 9, but we lost some big names to injuries last week, including Lamar Jackson (thumb) and Jeremiyah Love (ankle). Luckily for fantasy football managers, there are viable options to add off the waiver wire. Here are five players to consider adding who are still available in the majority of Yahoo leagues.

Darnold is one of the best quarterbacks to consider adding if you need a replacement for Jackson. He'll be on a short week after playing the Bears on Monday Night Football, but it doesn't get much better than a home matchup against the Cardinals. Arizona allows an NFL worst 29.8 points per game. Part of that can be attributed to them allowing 18 passing touchdowns to go along with just three interceptions. Darnold missed the first meeting with the Cardinals this season, but his backup Drew Lock emerged from the matchup with three touchdowns.

Through the first half of the season, A.J. Brown (knee/ankle) has been a massive disappointment. He was unable to play against the Dolphins on Sunday, marking his fifth missed game already. Even when he has played, he has looked limited. He hasn't surpassed three receptions in a game yet. Combine his injury issues with Romeo Doubs (shoulder) being on IR and Douglas has 17 receptions for 250 yards and four touchdowns over the last four games. Even if Brown plays against the Packers this week, Douglas still has fantasy upside.

In the first game since the Commanders lost Terry McLaurin (knee) for the rest of the season, Williams received a team-high seven targets versus the Eagles. He turned them into five receptions for 54 yards. Stefon Diggs provided the lone receiving touchdown in the game for Washington, and Treylon Burks also made an impact with three receptions for 81 yards. However, Williams having the most targets on the team makes him an intriguing option to consider in PPR formats for the second half of the season.

Sampson only received one carry versus the Steelers last week, but his rushing upside is not the reason to consider rostering him in fantasy. He is a great pass-catching option, and he showed it again with six receptions for 62 yards versus Pittsburgh. In two games since coming off IR, he caught nine of 11 targets for 94 yards. He is a viable flex option in fantasy for a Week 9 matchup with the Saints, who allow the third-most yards per target to running backs.

The 49ers had a bye last week, which was perfect timing because Christian McCaffrey suffered a mild hamstring injury against the Falcons in Week 7. With him missing the second half of that game, Black turned 12 carries into 63 yards and one touchdown. If McCaffrey ever misses time, Black could be a difference maker in fantasy. Even if you can't start him when McCaffrey is healthy, he's worth stashing on your bench in case an injury to the 49ers' superstar running back were to occur.

Darnold is one of the best quarterbacks to consider adding for Week 9, while Douglas and Williams are two wide receivers to target. Sampson's pass-catching upside makes him a viable running back to target, while it's worth stashing Black in the event of a McCaffrey injury. Be sure to check back throughout the season as we provide the top waiver wire options to help you win your fantasy football league.

#2828. Santoli: AI bull market almost surely needs tech stocks to recover to keep advancing

The road to bull-market glory does not run through Walmart and Berkshire Hathaway and Eli Lilly.

There's been a virtual buying panic in traditionally defensive, high-quality stocks over the past few weeks, a near-equal-and-opposite offset to the selling pressure in semiconductors and AI-build-out spenders. The three stocks above — the only members of the $1 trillion market-cap club not in Big Tech – have collectively added $550 billion in market value in the past month, almost making up for what Alphabet has lost.

The Nasdaq-100 and the S&P 500 Low Volatility ETF (SPLV) are rushing toward one another in a satisfying picture of symmetry that nonetheless reflects an almost comical level of tactical rotation executed by an investment community accustomed to playing one factor against another.

While this to-and-fro has so far protected the S&P 500 as a whole from an appreciable setback for months, it has left it stymied around levels that date back to May — before we got two earnings-reporting seasons showing 30% annual profit growth.

A week ago here, I suggested the AI theme was nearing a make-or-break moment to stage a recovery or risk surrendering its leadership status and destabilizing the broader market. Thus far, this trade has failed to respond to moderate oversold conditions or a run of cosmetically strong hyperscaler quarterly results, further loosening its grip on the benefit of the doubt.

In stressed markets undergoing soul-searching about the long-term bullish thesis, prices often need to travel further to find buyers with conviction while the market action must disappoint dip-buyers to extinguish complacency. Hard to know if we're there, but presumably we're now closer.

The Nasdaq-100 closed Monday right at its 200-day moving average, off almost 11% from its record high. After an enervating failed Monday-morning bounce attempt, the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index finished down another 3.8% and settled under the late-July low that was set as the Situational Awareness hedge fund was finished liquidating.

"Markets did not get the memo that bearish seasonal pressure typically subsides in mid-October and instead ended the month with some of the most acute selling pressure that has been realized during the late-summer/early-fall period," notes JPMorgan technical strategist Jason Hunter. He's advising clients to remain on the defensive "until we see clear evidence of a bottoming process on the charts or until there are clear signs of capitulation."

This is a time-tested playbook, though many a sharp market pullback has ended without what would qualify as unqualified capitulation.

The market action is winning some plaudits for the way non-tech stocks have held up better, many groups benefiting from the "quality" rotation while abandoned consumer cyclicals get a reprieve from easing oil prices and the slide in the 10-year Treasury yield back below 5%.

This gives active managers plenty to do in playing against a highly concentrated and tech-dependent S&P 500. But I'll continue to argue that the bull market built on the AI story will almost surely need the AI story to get back on track to keep advancing. A prolonged period when the equal-weight S&P 500 outperforms the market-cap-weighted version is otherwise known – usually – as a bear market.

The AI-related stocks' weighting in the S&P 500 is off the highs but remains near 45%, according to Morgan Stanley.

As the chart shows, AI has wobbled relative to the rest of the market during this uptrend before reasserting itself.  But there's a trickier element to the current gut check: In prior phases of the buildout, the key limiting bottleneck gave investors something to buy in order to exploit it.

The initial scarcity of GPUs meant to own Nvidia above all else from 2023 through 2024. When cloud capacity, power and cooling were found to be the crucial scarce resources, hyperscaler stocks worked and the AI-adjacent industrials were drastically revalued. Then memory emerged as the chokepoint and Micron and Sandisk went stratospheric.

Now, as Nvidia's Jensen Huang has said, capital is the constraint and that not only leaves no asset to buy, but it raises the cost of capital broadly as the mega-caps go free-cash-flow-negative.

Overlapping feedback loops and scrambled correlations across the markets and the real economy have further sapped investor conviction. Treasury yields no longer move with oil but largely with AI-capex sentiment, with both in retreat lately. AI-stock setbacks also mute inflation momentum and the equity wealth effect, meaning fewer Federal Reserve rate hikes are anticipated now versus a month ago.

This all probably sounds like an elaborate trap, and it has become one for the previously crowded trades. Could this reflect the end game for the AI-exuberance market chapter, which as I keep pointing out peaked around the SpaceX IPO in June? Impossible to say for sure, but if it were, I'm not sure things would look much different to this point.

But I don't want to dismiss the possibility that a lot of the known challenges are largely reflected in prices.

I argued in August that we should assume the valuation peak for this cycle was in. We've now retrenched to a forward P/E of 18.5 on the S&P 500 from 23 a year ago and the Nasdaq-100 at 20x is at the slimmest premium to the S&P 500 valuation in nine years. I'm on board with the idea that the market is telling us companies are now "over-earning," but if nothing else there is less valuation risk now than in recent months.

Sentiment toward AI stocks is in the dumps. Funds' positioning in tech has retreated below neutral. Important trading lows this year have happened near the turn of the month on March 30 and July 29, and the Nasdaq-100's relative-strength reading is exactly as oversold as it was at those turns.

So, stay alert for possible relief bounces that could evolve into something more consequential if indeed the midterm elections pass in a way that allows traders to unclench a bit into the year's home stretch.

John Kolovos of Macro Risk Advisors on the latest reading: "Similar to valuation, sentiment is not a timing tool, but it is getting harder and harder to find any bulls, as a near-perfect storm of tight financial conditions is being met with tech earnings that have gone from bad to worse in the blink of an eye."

-Another good big-picture conversation on the practical business and investment implications of the next phase of AI proliferation on Patrick O'Shaughnessy's Invest Like the Best podcast, this one with investor Cliff Sosin. His comparison of AI infrastructure economics to internet connectivity providers and his argument for how most of the productivity boost will go toward "consumer surplus" rather than corporate profits are particularly interesting.

-For my birthday Monday, my colleague and friend Sara Eisen wondered how I would write a birthday message to myself. I immediately regretted suggesting she ask ChatGPT, which generated this credible and somewhat embarrassing result:

"Another year older, though the longer-term trend remains constructive. Some modest deterioration in the underlying internals, perhaps, but nothing that suggests a decisive breakdown.

"The candles on the cake are approaching historically elevated levels, though valuation concerns have persisted for years without derailing the advance. At this stage, the key question isn't whether I've gotten older — that's largely priced in — but whether the wisdom gains can continue to offset the inevitable compression in energy multiples.

"For now, I'm maintaining a cautiously optimistic outlook, with upside optionality, downside support from family and friends, and no immediate need for Fed intervention. Happy birthday to me. The secular bull market remains intact."

The stampede into perceived defensive stocks has been a crucial driver of Apple shares' massive outperformance over the Nasdaq-100 since the AI-capex agita flared up months ago. Apple heading into Monday's earnings report had outperformed the Nasdaq-100 by nearly 20 percentage points over the prior six months.

Its singular refusal to join the aggressive spending binge to build AI capacity preserved its free cash flow and made it the perfect counterweight to the capex trade, while inflating Apple's valuation to a 15-year high.

But its advantage in free-cash-flow yield has now narrowed, thanks to its stock-price gains and the collapse of peer shares.

#2823. Luka Doncic's 42-point effort carries Lakers past Giannis Antetokounmpo and Heat

The clash between Luka Doncic and Giannis Antetokounmpo received top billing Monday night at Crypto.com Arena.

Doncic leads the league in scoring with 37.7 points per game for the Lakers, and Antetokounmpo is fifth at 28.2 for the Miami Heat.

But this matchup didn't quite live up to the hype after Doncic continued his early season dominance in the Lakers' 138-117 win.

Doncic was masterful, producing 42 points, 13 assists and seven rebounds. He was 14 for 24 from the field and six for 15 from three-point range.

Read more: 'Like a supernova.' Luka Doncic's 45 points only eclipsed by his defense in Lakers' win

"Feels good. Like I said, obviously when we win, it feels way better," Doncic said. "And that's the only important stuff. But obviously it feels good. You know, when you win by [21] against a team like Miami, it's really awesome, especially on a back-to-back."

Antetokounmpo finished with 30 points, 12 rebounds and seven assists, but he also had seven turnovers as he battled tight defensive coverage.

"I think we did OK. We did good enough to get the win," said Ziaire Williams, who played after missing three games because of soreness in his left foot. "Wasn't perfect. He's a freak of nature, man. It takes three, four guys loading up to him. One-on-one, he'll go through anybody in this league, and that's just the truth."

Doncic was better, but he had more help. Austin Reaves scored 27 points on 10-for-15 shooting and Walker Kessler finished with 15 points and 15 rebounds for his third consecutive double-double.

Miami Heat forward Giannis Antetokounmpo, right, scores over Lakers center Walker Kessler in the second half. (Robert Gauthier / Los Angeles Times)

"Yeah, he's a big piece for us," Doncic said about Kessler. "I think when he plays good, he gives us a way better chance to win. So, he just needs to keep going like this. Like we told him, we have his back. ... But what he's been doing, it's helping us win."

The Lakers (6-2) shot 57%, including 44% (19 for 43) from three-point range. They also notched 39 assists on 51 successful shots.

"The 39 assists, that's the most assists we've had since I've been the [coach]," JJ Redick said. "So, it's a really good offense that we're playing right now. And a lot of that, of course, is Doncic and what he's able to do and bend the defense. And his shot-making creates a lot of problems, and then he can make every pass."

Lakers guard Austin Reaves shoots over Miami Heat guard Davion Mitchell in the first half. (Robert Gauthier / Los Angeles Times)

Doncic has been on a scoring tear for the Lakers.

In the third quarter, Doncic missed a three-pointer, but Jake LaRavia hustled to collect the rebound. He passed the ball to Doncic, who then made a three to give the Lakers a 93-74 lead. The play forced the Heat (2-5) to call a timeout as Doncic pointed at LaRavia in appreciation.

Doncic started strong like usual, with 16 points, five assists and four rebounds in the first quarter as the Lakers took a 37-30 lead. Doncic, who was called for a technical foul late in the first half — his second of the season — had 24 points by halftime.

In his previous four games, Doncic had scored 45, 37, 41 and 52 points.

"Like the defensive mindset in me is always like, 'Why are they not blitzing him or why are they not doubling? Or why are they just letting him just walk to his spot and just do whatever he wants out there?' But they're trying," Williams said about defensive strategies Doncic faces. "He just is that good and he just makes it look that easy.

"He's unbelievable, man. Even a better person. And yeah, I'm happy to be on his side for sure."

#2822. Too many sacks for Deshaun Watson and Browns offense

The Browns' offense clearly didn't get it done against the Steelers. Sometimes, you look for the 'what ifs' to try to make yourself (as a fan) feel a little better about the loss. Overall, it seemed like Pittsburgh had a better physicality. But I would also look to the Browns being 0-of-3 in the red zone and 0-of-2 in goal-to-go situations. And of course, generally, you can't take 8 sacks as a quarterback and expect to move the ball consistently with success after that.

Below, we analyze the snap counts and stats on offense for the Cleveland Browns' Week 8 game against the Pittsburgh Steelers.

27-of-39 (69.2%) for 324 yards. 3 carries, 2 yards (0.7 YPC). 1 fumble lost

It's hard to imagine someone having the stat line that Deshaun Watson did, and yet the offense only ends up with two field goals. But Cleveland fumbling on the opening two possessions, and also seeing their quarterback sacked eight times, tells a lot more of the story. As does the fact that they were down 27-6 in the fourth quarter, resulting in a heavier mix of passes when it didn't matter.

Watson was the Browns' lowest-graded player by PFF, grading out to a 47.5. I think it was well-earned, to a degree. While Watson made some OK throws, some of the sacks came down to a lack of decisiveness. We're also seeing that, against better defensive fronts, Watson is not able to escape for yards, something that has bailed him out at times this year. At least Watson gets to face a much easier defense in the Saints next week.

1 carry, 7 yards (7.0 YPC). 6 catches, 62 yards (8 targets).

11 carries, 25 yards (2.3 YPC). 2 catches, 24 yards (2 targets).

With Cleveland falling behind early, the ground game couldn't become the factor that Todd Monken wanted it to be. They tried loading up with a full house formation on 3rd-and-1 on their opening first half drive, telling Pittsburgh, "we think we can get a yard when you know it's coming." Nope, it was killed by the defense. The same thing happened on first down from the 2 yard line, Quinshon Judkins stood no chance and was dropped for a loss of 3 yards.

Dylan Sampson was one of the Browns' higher-graded offensive players, grading out to a 67.4. He had 6 catches, although again, much of that came in garbage time.

Denzel Boston nearly made a great, one-handed touchdown grab, but couldn't get his second foot down in bounds.

I like what someone said before — it's not the best when Malachi Corley is getting 20% of the snaps, and yet never even gets targeted. It shouldn't even be an element of surprise thing, but there has to be some form of 'threat' that maybe it's going to be a pass play or something when he's in the game.

KC Concepcion only had 4 targets and 0 touches on the ground, a week after he had career highs. Jerry Jeudy also did not have a catch.

10 catches, 154 yards (12 targets). 1 fumble lost.

It was a career day for Harold Fannin, who had 10 catches for 154 yards. He did lose a fumble for the second week in a row, but remains a huge threat in the passing game. Fannin was the Browns' highest-graded player by PFF, grading out to a 76.4.

With how many sacks the Steelers had, let's turn to PFF to see how the unit did. RT Tytus Howard was the second-highest graded player on offense, grading to a 75.4. The third-highest? LG Zion Johnson with a 68.0. Fifth-highest? RG Austin Barber with a 65.4. C Elgton Jenkins and LT Spencer Fano did not fare the best, but it wasn't necessarily catastrophic.

Fano was probably the big disappointment on the day. But otherwise, it was what I'd call a mediocre day for the offensive line. They were responsible for some mistakes, but better quarterback play still could've made this a different ballgame.

#2819. New York Jets News: Insider Says Aaron Glenn Path to Salvation has Closed

It appears Glenn is a dead man walking. There are nine games left in the 2026 regular season for the Jets, and it's about to get ugly. The next two games include a road tilt against the Kansas City Chiefs and a home game against the Buffalo Bills.
