#2818. Game Recap: Booker fouls out as Suns fall short, 116-113

The Phoenix Suns fall to 1-4 on the season and have lost 3 straight games. There was progress overall, but the ultimate goal is to win games, and the Suns broke down late with key turnovers and missed free throws.

The Suns led by as many as 17 points, survived a Draymond Green ejection and a Stephen Curry heater, and still lost it at the free-throw line.

Golden State won 116-113, dropping Phoenix to 1-4. The Suns won the first half at the stripe 13-of-14 to 8-of-10, then watched the Warriors go 23-of-27 in the second half to their 9-of-16. Devin Booker fouled out with under a minute left and Phoenix down five.

Devin Booker led Phoenix with 26 points on 8-of-18 shooting, 2-of-4 from three and 8-of-8 at the line, with 5 assists in 34:58 before fouling out.

Miles Bridges added 21 points on 7-of-14 shooting, 3-of-5 from three, with 6 rebounds and 4 steals. Dillon Brooks scored 18 on 5-of-8 shooting with 8 rebounds and 4 assists. Oso Ighodaro was perfect at 6-of-6 for 14 points off the bench, and Jalen Green filled it up with 11 points, 8 rebounds and 8 assists. Luke Kennard finished +15 in 17:42.

Stephen Curry led all scorers with 34 points on 9-of-21 shooting, 5-of-13 from three and 11-of-12 at the line, with 7 assists and 6 rebounds. Brandin Podziemski added 20, Charles Bassey had 19 points and 8 rebounds off the bench, and Yaxel Lendeborg scored 16 with 8-of-9 at the line.

The Suns opened with Devin Booker, Dillon Brooks, Jalen Green, Miles Bridges, and Man Man, and the defense set the tone right away. Golden State's starting five went a combined 3-of-14 from the floor for 8 points.

The Warriors lived behind the arc and paid for it. Golden State went 2-of-15 from three, including Stephen Curry at 1-of-4, while Phoenix hit 2 of its 5 attempts. The Suns shot 60% overall (12-of-20) and turned Warriors mistakes into 9 points off turnovers and 8 fast-break points, compared to 3 and 2 for Golden State.

The bench kept the pressure on. Oso Ighodaro went a perfect 3-of-3 from the field and 2-of-2 at the line for 8 points, the most of anyone on the floor. Phoenix closed the quarter with Bridges, Luke Kennard, Jordan Goodwin, Collin Gillespie and Ighodaro, and Kennard posted a +9 in under four minutes without scoring.

The only area Golden State matched Phoenix was inside, where both teams scored 20 points in the paint. Charles Bassey led the Warriors with 7 points off the bench, and the Warriors won second-chance points 6-5. Booker led the Suns at +10 with 5 points and 2 assists, and Brooks added 5 points on perfect shooting.

Phoenix led after the first quarter, 31-19. A drastic first quarter improvement defensively from the opening 4 games.

After a 2-of-15 first quarter, the Warriors went 5-of-9 from three in the second and shot 50% overall (10-of-20). Yaxel Lendeborg hit both of his threes for 8 points in the quarter, and Brandin Podziemski added 6 on a pair of triples.

The Suns lost their grip on the paint. After winning the paint 16-12 in the first, Phoenix went 2-of-8 inside in the second for 4 points while Golden State scored 10 there. The Warriors also won the quarter on the boards 11-5.

Devin Booker kept Phoenix ahead. He scored 11 in the quarter on 3-of-4 shooting and a perfect 4-of-4 at the line, and the Suns went 8-of-8 at the stripe as a team. Luke Kennard knocked down two threes for 6 points off the bench, and Miles Bridges chipped in 7.

The Suns pushed the lead to 16 at 55-39 with 3:45 left in the half. Then the offense stalled. Golden State ripped off a 12-0 run over the next 2:42, cutting it to 55-51 at the 1:03 mark.

Golden State won the second quarter 32-26.

Phoenix led 57-51 at the break. Booker paced the Suns with 16 points on 5-of-8 shooting and 5-of-5 at the line. Bridges added 11 points and 5 rebounds, and Jalen Green posted a team-best +10 with four boards.

There was a huge blow to start the second half, as the Suns announced Jordan Goodwin would not return due to a calf injury.

As you'd expect, Dillon Brooks and Draymond Green got into it.

Draymond threw Khaman Maluach to the ground to start the altercation, and Dillon Brooks went to pull him back from heading towards Khaman who was on the ground. Green grabbed Brooks by his neck before the situation de-escalated.

Green was assessed a double technical and ejected, and Brooks received a technical.

Green played just two minutes in the third before his exit. He finished with 4 points on 1-of-2 shooting, 4 rebounds and 2 assists in 16:46.

Losing Green didn't slow Golden State. Quite the opposite, actually. Phoenix did not have an answer for Charles Bassey, who had 6 points and 4 boards off the bench in the quarter. Stephen Curry took over with 18 points in the quarter on 6-of-9 shooting and 4-of-5 from three. Brandin Podziemski added 10, and the Warriors shot 59.1% in the period.

The free-throw line flipped on Phoenix. After going 13-of-14 in the first half, the Suns went 6-of-12 in the third. Jalen Green missed all four of his attempts.

Brooks kept the Suns afloat with 9 points on 3-of-3 shooting, and Man Man went 2-of-2 for 5 points. Jalen Green dished 5 assists in the quarter. Devin Booker scored 8 but went 2-of-7 and picked up four fouls in the period.

Tied at 93, the fourth turned into a grind. Golden State made just 5 field goals in the quarter on 5-of-17 shooting, and Stephen Curry went 1-of-5. It didn't matter. The Warriors lived at the line, going 12-of-13 in the period while Phoenix went 3-of-4.

The Suns won the glass 13-8 and grabbed five offensive rebounds, but gave it right back with seven turnovers. Jalen Green battled for 7 points and 4 rebounds, three of them offensive, but had three of those turnovers. Phoenix went 1-of-7 from three in the quarter.

Booker fouled out on a flop from De'Anthony Melton, called for an offensive foul with under a minute left and Phoenix down 113-108. He finished with 26 points on 8-of-18 shooting, 8-of-8 at the line and 5 assists, but managed just 2 points on 1-of-3 shooting in the fourth.

Golden State won the fourth quarter 23-20 and the game 116-113.

The Suns will host the Blazers team that destroyed them on Opening Night on Wednesday in Downtown Phoenix.

#2814. John Harbaugh admits calling Amy Adams Strunk about Jeffery Simmons trade

Giants coach John Harbaugh apparently ruffled some feathers in Nashville by making a phone call to Titans owner Amy Adams Strunk in an effort to spark trade discussions for defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons. And Harbaugh doesn’t care about any “code” that he may have violated by going over the head of Titans G.M. Mike Borgonzi.

Harbaugh admitted to reporters on Monday that he made the call. And he vowed to make similar calls, if it’s in the best interests of the team.

“I did, I did,” Harbaugh said regarding whether he called Strunk. “I left a message. . . . Because you turn over every stone. You know, gonna try everything. I mean, I just, I’ll put it out there, man, we’re open for any call. Anybody wants to call us or call anybody. Call.”

Next came a discussion about songs that send the “call me” message, from the Blondie song of that name to the more recent Call Me Maybe.

“Let’s put that out there,” Harbaugh said. “Put that out there. It was kind of, like, fun to see the outpouring of interest in that. I enjoyed it. We’re not like — we’re not part of any secret society oath that says you can only talk to somebody. We’re not in that. We didn’t get memoed on that.  We’re not in that agreement. So like I said, anybody can call us. We won’t be adhering to that particular agreement.”

Amen to all of that. The only rules that should matter are the written rules. Those who tiptoe around unwritten rules undermine their chances to get shit done.

Besides, calls between teams happen at many different levels. There isn’t an exclusive G.M.-to-G.M. network of bat phones.

In this specific case, the Giants apparently weren’t getting anywhere with Borgonzi. So they got creative, and resourceful.

As we understand it, they didn’t hear from the Titans to see if they’d match or beat the offer the Bears made for Simmons. Which would seem to be the sensible thing to do before finalizing a deal — unless the Bears expressly set a firm and quick take-it-or-leave-it deadline that prevented the Titans from exploring whether more could have been gotten from another team.

#2813. The Cardinals’ running back situation: Highs and lows

The Arizona Cardinals went into training camp with one position group that was deemed to be no problem: the running back room.

Against the Dallas Cowboys, a 33-30 victory on the road, the only healthy running back that remained on the roster during the game was veteran Tyler Allgeier. This ended up being a good thing because he balled out for 147 yards, scored two touchdowns, and was a key component of the win.

Rookie Jeremiyah Love was deemed ready to play, but re-injured an ankle. Bam Morris, the game's RB3, took a solid hit to the helmet from Cowboys LB DeMarvion Overshown and had to leave the game. Morris fumbled on the play with 21 seconds remaining in the first half. Dallas then drove into field goal range, but their kicker hooked it right.

Neither running back returned to the game. This forced every carry onto the shoulders of Allgeier. In the game, he scored on a 63-yard run with :57 left in the third quarter, and on his next carry reeled off a 52-yarder. Which is all well and good, but after a long run like this, usually, the running back goes to the sideline and rests for a few plays.

Not in this game. Allgeier stepped right back into the huddle. The next play called was Allgeier up the middle for four yards. So much for a well-deserved break.

So, what now? Surely, the Cardinals can't go into every contest with a single running back.

In training camp, the position group was viewed as a stacked room full of really good talent. There were suggestions and rumors that perhaps Arizona should trade a guy.

James Conner was thought to be close to being ready to come back to the practice field. Same for Trey Benson. Bam Knight was re-signed in March from last year's roster to a one-year deal. Corey Kiner was still under contract. Then, GM Monti Ossenfort selected Love with the third overall pick in the NFL Draft.

Allgeier was one of the "splash" moves in free agency. He was seen as the new starting running back after several successful seasons with the Atlanta Falcons before Love was drafted. In August, the team was awarded Evan Hull off waivers from the Houston Texans, and he played well in preseason. Tre Stewart was signed in mid-August.

The running back room was bursting with players and talent. Love, Allgeier, Conner, and Benson were viewed as a spectacular display of talent. Knight would most likely become a kickoff returner.

Kiner was traded to the New England Patriots. Stewart was a last-day cut from training camp. Conner was placed on IR. Hull was signed to the practice squad. Benson was waived, then reverted to the team after he cleared waivers and was placed on the IR/injury list.

So, the Cardinals began the season with Love as the starter, Allgeier as his backup, and Knight as RB3, with Hull on the practice squad. Meanwhile, the team kept a close eye on Conner's recovery and hoped that one day he could return. With Love always a question mark, the coaches brought up Hull as insurance, but against Dallas, he was listed as inactive. Apparently, nobody consulted their crystal ball first.

Knight has a concussion, which is good news. The bad side is that this is usually 10 days before the player is reactivated. So, against the Seattle Seahawks, Knight won't be available.

Now Love? All of us know he has had ankle issues since getting hurt in a preseason game. Since that time, he has injured both ankles. Against the Cowboys, Love suffered a new injury: a higher ankle sprain. Several media outlets are suggesting that, because Love keeps getting hurt with the same type of injury, the Cardinals should just shut him down, place him on IR, and allow him time to completely heal for next year.

That leaves Allgeier and Hull to play out the schedule.

This same scenario happened last year. All of the good starting backs became injured, and the team crawled their way towards the end of the schedule with practice squad running backs.

Isn't that what Arizona is dangerously close to becoming? If Allgeier becomes injured, the team's run game will lie solely on Hull, a practice squad guy. Attrition happens to every team and is the resident evil during the back half of every NFL season.

For one, already on the Cardinals' practice squad is veteran Jerome Ford, age 27. Elevate him now.

Ford (5'-10", 210 pounds) was a fifth-round draft pick of the Cleveland Browns in 2022. This was the time frame when Cleveland had both Nick Chubb, Kareem Hunt, and D'Ernest Johnson. Ford made the final roster and returned kickoffs.

The next season, Chubb sustained a horrible knee injury, and Ford split carries with Hunt and had a career-high 813 yards on 204 carries with four TDs. He played four seasons with the Browns, so he has experience. During his career, he had 107 receptions for 647 yards, so he has been involved in the short passing game.

That will give Arizona Allgeier, Ford as his backup, and Hull at RB3.

Veteran Cam Akers remains unsigned and would be a good backup. He has played in 59 NFL games with 17 starts. Cardinals fans will remember him from the Los Angeles Rams. Trayveon Williams was a sixth-round pick by the Cincinnati Bengals in 2019 and played 75 games in seven years.

Or maybe Ossenfort can scour every team's practice squad and find a healthy body. Almost every team has a minimum of one running back. Several teams have numerous RBs.

#2811. Todd Monken expects to see sacks start to "pile up" for Jared Verse

Browns defensive end Jared Verse made a fair amount of noise heading into Sunday’s game against the Steelers, but he was mostly silent on the field in Pittsburgh.

Verse had one tackle in the 27-6 Steelers win and that continued the slow start to his Browns tenure. Verse had 8.5 sacks and 34 quarterback hits in 20 total games for the Rams in 2025, but had no sacks and six quarterback hits since coming to Cleveland in the Myles Garrett trade.

“Well, I think it’s too early to say anything. Be ahead in games, force the other team to throw it, and I think you’ll start to see his sacks start to pile up.”

The Browns have a 5-3 record despite the lack of contributions from Verse, but their hopes of making a playoff push over the second half would feel stronger if he starts making more headlines with his play than his locker room quotes.

#2810. ESPN honors Chris Spatola with tribute during Army-Duke broadcast

The death of Chris Spatola was felt across sports media when the tragic news broke in late September.

Spatola, a beloved college basketball analyst at ESPN, passed away unexpectedly on Sept. 21, according to his obituary, and is survived by his wife, Jamie, and their three children. He was 47.

The lasting grief permeated ESPN's broadcast during halftime of Duke's season-opening win over Army on Monday.

After a touching 70-second video montage, ESPN cameras panned to Spatola's family in attendance at Cameron Indoor Stadium.

"Chris gone at 47," ESPN play-by-play announcer Dave O'Brien said. "His family in the stands. Many of them here, like Jamie right there, for Army and Duke. Jay Bilas, more than a colleague for you. This was a friend, a very close friend."

"Close friend," Bilas said. "Chris Spatola—pardon my language—was a bada**. He served our country as a decorated combat veteran. He was an outstanding player at West Point. He was a four-year starter, scored over 1,500 points, and was an outstanding coach here under Mike Krzyzewski. Helped Duke win a national championship in 2010 with Jon Scheyer as Duke's point guard for that championship season."

Spatola married Jamie, Krzyzewski's daughter, in 2004. After serving five years in the United States Army, Krzyzewski hired Spatola in 2007, quickly earning a promotion to director of basketball operations in May 2008. Spatola remained on the Duke men's basketball staff through 2012, and he joined ESPN in 2015.

"But Chris was an incredibly thoughtful human being and well read. He and his wife, Jamie, just a beautiful couple. A devoted husband, father. I just can't say enough positive things about him. He was selfless in every way.

"You know, Dave, we both worked with him and knew him. Chris never led with his accomplishments. He had an uncommon humility to him. For him, it was about the players and the coaches and all that. He wasn't talking about himself, and he had a lot to talk about.

"But just an extraordinary human being and miss him terribly. He always kept his wife, Jamie, his three children, first and foremost."

Bilas shared that he's "talked at length" with Spatola's friends since his passing, with Spatola's selflessness serving as the through line in those conversations.

"You mention a phrase, [and] I think it's right on the money: uncommon humility," O'Brien said. "My own son, Mike, was serving in Afghanistan at the time that I was working several games with Chris, and we would talk about it. I would pass along some of my son's stories. He was absolutely fascinated by those."

O'Brien punctuated the segment by reiterating how deeply Spatola will be missed in the ESPN family and beyond.

#2809. Cowboys news: Defense banged up physically after collapse to Cardinals

The Dallas Cowboys are licking their wounds after another bad loss at home to the Arizona Cardinals, and are starting the week of preparation for the Indianapolis Colts with a slew of injuries to monitor. The team's state of play in the first half of the season has produced a losing record that's going to be even harder to climb back from now.

Another theme coming out of the Cardinals loss is that perfectly healthy players, mainly George Pickens and CeeDee Lamb, didn't see the field at critical moments.

It's been a quiet few weeks for the Cowboys second first-round pick, and now Malachi Lawrence may be shelved for a few weeks ahead.

On Tuesday, Cowboys coach Brian Schottenheimer announced that rookie OLB Malachi Lawrence suffered an ankle injury against the Cardinals and could miss multiple weeks.

"Malachi has [an injury] that might be a little bit longer…" Schottenheimer said.

"Looks like it could be a few weeks. So just keep doing some more testing, see how he moves around, but a little different than the other guys."

The Cowboys still can't defend big plays, and now have two safeties in concussion protocol to start the week.

Coach Brian Schottenheimer said defensive back Caleb Downs and safety P.J. Locke are both in concussion protocol.

Downs, a first-round rookie, has played 509 defensive snaps — 92 percent — and 45 on special teams. He has totaled 52 tackles, a sack, a pass defensed and two forced fumbles.

Locke just returned from injured reserve, missing five games with a foot injury. He returned to practice Oct. 23.

The Cowboys are still open to adding players under Brian Schottenheimer, despite the stunning lack of consistency defining his tenure right now.

The Cowboys are 25 games into the Brian Schottenheimer era, and they have a losing record in that stretch. Part of the reason is they've strung together a winning streak only once in those 25 games, when they won three in a row last November.

Other than that, they haven't been able to stack wins. Schottenheimer said it himself earlier this season: good teams stack wins.

He said that heading into the Tampa Bay Buccaneers game. Since then, the Cowboys have failed to do it, even with a favorable stretch.

It is still really hard to fathom how the Cowboys managed to give Sunday's game away.

But let's be real: This was an epic collapse, for which Dallas should be crushed.

On Sunday, a confluence of ineptitude caused Dallas to collapse at home to drop to 3-5 on the season. The defense became a sieve in the fourth quarter and couldn't tackle. I understand that the Cowboys had some injuries, but letting Allgeier look like Jim Brown is not a product of a few missing players. The secondary inexplicably got burned late when all the Cowboys needed to do was keep everything in front of them. Frankly, Arizona could have scored quicker in the fourth quarter if not for a few misses. The defensive breakdowns continue to perplex Dallas this season.

The Cardinals played two different QBs and RBs and racked up yards at will against the Cowboys, while the home team couldn't execute their own offense.

For the second straight week, George Pickens was largely an afterthought.

Ten targets over the last two weeks simply isn't enough for a player with his skill set. Pickens finished with two catches for 48 yards, including a 37-yard reception, and appeared to have a 59-yard touchdown wiped off the board by a holding penalty. Even after proving he could challenge Arizona vertically, Dallas never made a concerted effort to get him more involved.

The front seven has been a work in progress in the three games since Mike Pettine was added to the coaching staff.

Things are getting interesting at linebacker. Shemar James played almost every snap, marking his third straight week with over three-quarters of the defense's reps. Rookie Jaishawn Barham has taken the biggest hit as a result of James's ascension; his action on just 20% of the defensive snaps was easily his lowest as a Cowboy (and less than even Von Miller's intentionally spotty workload). James Houston also got a bump in usage; his 20 snaps with the defense were his most of 2026.

#2808. Ravens QB Joe Fagnano "fighting through a couple things," has "a chance" to play Thursday

The Ravens are short on healthy quarterbacks with Thursday’s game against the Jaguars on the horizon.

Lamar Jackson is headed to injured reserve with a thumb injury that requires surgery and could end his season. Tyler Huntley had to leave Sunday’s game as well and is dealing with a toe injury that has Joe Fagnano in line to make his first NFL start.

Fagnano’s availability sounds like less than a sure thing, however. He took a low hit from Bills defensive tackle Ed Oliver in the second half of the Ravens loss and head coach Jesse Minter didn’t commit to him making the start this week.

“We’re kinda working through some things,” Minter said. “To Joe’s credit, he’s kinda fighting through a couple things. Expect him to have a chance to play Thursday.”

The Ravens signed Austin Reed to their practice squad on Monday and he could find himself in the mix for playing time during his first days with the team.

#2807. Knicks Notes: Mike Brown not hesitating to sit Mikal Bridges; Jordan Clarkson providing spark

Mikal Bridges was 1-for-7 in the third quarter on Monday and the Knicks were struggling. Some NBA head coaches would stick with their veteran in that spot. It's easier to keep a player like Bridges -- one who is signed to a large contract and was obtained for significant trade capital -- on the court.

But in this respect, Mike Brown isn't most coaches. He didn't hesitate to bench Bridges in the second half of the Knicks' win over the Nets.

We're seven games into the season. Brown has pulled Bridges in the third quarter of three of those seven games.

"Yeah, he's just got to keep playing. Everybody goes through those ups and downs throughout the course of the season, and obviously, it's the beginning of the season right now, so he's got to find his way," Brown said after New York's win over Brooklyn. "Not just with him but with everybody, when you're in a funk you gotta find one or two things to hold your hat on. Mikal is one of the best runners in the NBA.

"In both directions -- offensively and defensively. So he's just got to try to hang his hat on that as well as his cutting and trying to do the other little things -- rebounding and driving and touching the paint and spraying it. Then this shot will come eventually."

Bridges is shooting 30 percent from the floor so far this year. If you've watched the Knicks during Bridges' tenure, you know that he can be up and down over the course of the regular season. And you also know he can deliver in big playoff situations. Brown believes Bridges will ultimately be fine.

"It's a matter of time in my opinion, before Mikal gets going," Brown said.

But if the Knicks head coach thinks someone else gives the team a better chance to win on a given night, he won't hesitate to sit Bridges.

After a sluggish three quarters, Brown turned to Jordan Clarkson and Tyler Kolek to start the fourth quarter. Clarkson, Kolek, Josh Hart, OG Anunoby and Karl-Anthony Towns sparked the Knicks early in the fourth.

It was the second time in the past three games that Clarkson helped turn things around. The veteran had 18 points in New York's comeback win in Chicago. But he has also basically been out of the rotation in three of New York's seven games.

"I think it shows his character more than anything else. He's someone that if he was on a different team, he could play 20, 25 minutes every night. He could score double digits. He could be that spark, that microwave off the bench. And he's here," Hart said. "He was willing to sacrifice and take a smaller role, be a part of something bigger than himself. So it shows its character.

"It shows his professionalism. To be able to go out and do those things. And when he's not doing it, he's out there coaching, not on the court, he's coaching guys up, talking to them, letting them know what he sees. So he's the ultimate professional. He's someone that you want on your team. And whenever he's given an opportunity, he'll go out and play well."

Brown also had effusive praise for Clarkson.

"You have a veteran that's been there, done that. and he's willing to accept the role that most guys wouldn't accept - even guys younger that haven't proved as much as he's proven in this league. And he's willing to accept it," Brown said. "So, in my opinion, if he's willing to accept it, which is what he did last year, and still try to uplift everybody, connect everybody while he's going through what he's going through, then everybody else should. That's that's the ultimate team player, I will say this about JC. I've been around some good teammates. He's got to be the best. He is the definition of ultimate pro."

Additionally, Landry Shamet led the team in scoring with 22 points off of the bench in 29 minutes. The veteran shot 4-for-6 from three, 6-for-8 from the field, and was a perfect 6-for-6 on free throws.

#2806. Cowboys DBs Caleb Downs, P.J. Locke in concussion protocol

The Cowboys had injury to go with the insult of Sunday’s 33-30 meltdown overtime loss to the Cardinals.

Coach Brian Schottenheimer said defensive back Caleb Downs and safety P.J. Locke are both in concussion protocol.

Downs, a first-round rookie, has played 509 defensive snaps — 92 percent — and 45 on special teams. He has totaled 52 tackles, a sack, a pass defensed and two forced fumbles.

Locke just returned from injured reserve, missing five games with a foot injury. He returned to practice Oct. 23.

Schottenheimer also said outside linebacker Malachi Lawrence has an ankle injury “that might be a little bit longer” to recover from. Lawrence, the team’s other first-round rookie, has played 226 defensive snaps and 40 in special teams.

He has totaled 13 tackles, a sack and two passes defensed.

#2805. Ben Johnson continues streak of electric postgame speeches as Bears surge

Ben Johnson's Chicago Bears notched a statement road victory over the Seattle Seahawks on Monday Night Football. They now sit at 6-2 and are in firm control of their destiny in a stacked NFC North.

Vibes couldn't be higher after the team was able to negotiate Caleb Williams' injury and come out the other side looking like a legitimate Super Bowl contender.

MORE: Super Bowl champ Shannon Sharpe sees no limits for Caleb Williams after Bears' 34-17 win over Seahawks

The Bears' dominant 34-17 victory featured surgical and balanced offense, plus a standout performance from Jeffery Simmons. Fans are waking up Tuesday with a pep in their steps as they consider just how complete the team looks—and how difficult of an out the Bears figure to be come playoff time.

And since there's no point in winning big if you're not going to celebrate accordingly, Johnson was primed to put a capper on the perfect night with a fired-up speech in the victorious locker room.

Johnson brought the energy but stopped short of taking his shirt off. His message? That the Bears didn't come out to the Pacific Northwest to send a message. They came to build something and that project is just getting started.

The charismatic young coach then punctuated his speech with a dance move that probably has a description yet all you need to know is that it's awesome.

#2804. Bengals Tuesday Trenches: Pros and cons of a potential Jonathan Taylor trade and more

The Cincinnati Bengals are 5-2 for the first time since the 2021 season and are very much in the mix for the AFC North title as we near the midway point of the season.

They're fresh off two needlessly exciting games, both of which saw them fall behind 13-0 before coming back to win. Their most recent cardiac-inducing victory came over the Tennessee Titans in overtime by a score of 43-36.

While they came out on top in the end, they also lost several players to injury and will likely need to address multiple positions before the trade deadline, whether through trades or free agency.

The Colts are going to trade Jonathan Taylor. It's going to happen, and the Bengals are reportedly one of five or six teams "aggressively pursuing" a possible trade for Taylor, according to Jordan Schultz.

So, before we get into some thoughts on Sunday's game and the upcoming matchup in Spain, here are some pros and cons of a possible trade for Taylor.

The first is obvious—Jonathan Taylor is a stud of a running back, and he would immediately upgrade the Bengals' rushing attack. The Bengals have the second-fewest rushing yards of any team in the NFL and average just 3.7 yards per carry. The coaching staff has desperately tried to create a balanced offense, putting Burrow under center more often than ever before. It's just not working. Defenses can focus on Burrow, his receivers, and the passing game without giving much thought to the rushing attack. If the Bengals trade for Taylor, that changes, and the offense should be even better because of it.

The Bengals also wouldn't need to worry about replacing Chase Brown after the 2026 season. Brown and Samaje Perine are both in contract years, and Taylor, a true three-down workhorse, could replace them both moving forward.

He would take an insane amount of pressure off Burrow. Right now, even as the Bengals try to force a running game, the offense flows through Burrow and his elite receivers. They've only scored four rushing touchdowns, and two of those belong to Burrow. Can you imagine if defenses couldn't drop five or six defensive backs into coverage to deal with Chase and Higgins because they actually had to worry about Taylor? What happens when defensive backs have to creep up in run support and safeties can no longer bracket Chase? Taylor would instantly make the Bengals' offense much less predictable.

And he's a workhorse. Brown averages only about 13 carries per game, while Perine gets around five. Taylor is capable of handling 20-plus carries per game and has proven he can shoulder the load of an NFL rushing attack.

The defense needs all the attention right now. Had we talked about this a couple of weeks ago, I'd have been all on board. That's not the case anymore, though. The secondary is dealing with injuries and inconsistent play, and linebacker depth is a concern. Outside of the defensive line, the Bengals should seriously consider adding a veteran cornerback or linebacker, especially if DJ Turner is going to miss significant time.

The cost would be enormous. The Bengals are already on the hook for Burrow's $275 million, Chase's $161 million, and Higgins' $115 million contracts. The last thing they need is to add Taylor's $44 million deal to the books. While it would be fun to see Taylor line up behind or next to Burrow, they don't need to spend that kind of money on a running back when the offense will still flow through Burrow and his receivers more often than not. Taylor makes more sense for a team with a quarterback who needs a strong running game to help carry the offense. There is a possible out after the 2026 season, but it would still cost the Bengals $10 million in dead money.

The running back position is devalued in 2026. While there are elite backs capable of doing elite things, some of the NFL's best offenses have relative nobodies carrying the football. In the 2020s, it's all about moving the ball through the air.

A running back's shelf life is also relatively short, which is part of the reason the position has been devalued in recent years. It's a punishing position, and bell-cow running backs typically don't last very long. The Derrick Henrys of the world are few and far between. Taylor is 27 now, and there's no guarantee he'll hold up to heavy use into his early 30s.

The trade compensation could also be more than the Bengals are willing to pay. If there really are several teams aggressively pursuing Taylor, the Bengals could find themselves in a bidding war, forcing them to surrender more draft capital than they're comfortable giving up.

Again, had this conversation taken place even a week earlier, I'd have been all for adding an elite running back to an already ridiculously overpowered offense.

The Bengals need help at cornerback first and foremost. Once they address that, they could still look at adding a veteran linebacker or another edge rusher to help replace Cashius Howell.

Taylor would be a luxury the Bengals don't really need and definitely can't afford moving forward.

I just don't think he gets them any closer to hoisting the Lombardi Trophy than Chase Brown does in 2026, and I certainly don't think they want to pay him the more than $21 million he'd be owed in 2027.

Here are some random thoughts on the Bengals' overtime win over the Titans:

The Dexter Lawrence sack late in the game that pushed the Titans out of field goal range was exactly why the Bengals traded away a top-10 pick to acquire him. That was an elite play from an elite player at a pivotal moment in the game. Had he not made that sack, the Bengals could be 4-3 today.

Teams continue to do whatever they can to bracket Chase, taking their chances with Higgins at their own peril. Higgins finished the game with 11 receptions for 155 yards and two touchdowns. Once the Titans tried to adjust, Chase got going, too, finishing with 11 catches for 141 yards and a touchdown. Pick your poison, I guess.

When the defensive line can't generate pressure on the quarterback, the secondary struggles to lock down receivers. That's why trading for a cornerback could be the best move the Bengals make before the deadline.

This is the kind of game the 2024 and 2025 Bengals lose.

Burrow was both brilliant and weirdly reckless on Sunday. He completed 38 of 48 passes for 462 yards and five touchdowns. However, he also threw three interceptions, a couple of which were pretty ugly, including an underthrow of a wide-open Chase that should have been a touchdown. As long as he's brilliant more often than he's reckless, I guess it doesn't matter.

Boye Mafe has been an outstanding signing.

The defense's inability to tackle continues to be a major issue as the Bengals head into Week 9. At this point, what needs to change?

The running game wasn't necessarily ineffective against the Titans, but it was underutilized. Brown averaged 4.6 yards per carry on Sunday, his second-highest average of the season, but carried the ball just 12 times. They were in a shootout, though, so I get it.

Five of the Titans' seven losses have come by six points or fewer. They're better than their 1-7 record suggests.

That being said, the Bengals are the much better team, and I would have liked to see them dominate Tennessee instead of allowing Cam Ward to throw for more than 300 yards for the first time in his career.

If you're still with me, the Bengals travel to Spain to face the Atlanta Falcons on Sunday morning as part of the league's slate of international games this season.

The Falcons are 3-5 and are coming off a bizarre shutout loss to the Buccaneers in which Tampa Bay had ZERO passing yards.

The Falcons are better than the Titans, and the Bengals can't afford to let them jump out to a 13-0 lead the way the Ravens and Titans did over the last two weeks.

That kind of football isn't sustainable, and the Bengals need to count their blessings that they managed to come away with wins in both games.

With this being an international game, everything is going to be thrown out of whack. The Bengals leave for Spain on Tuesday (today!) to adjust to the time change before stepping onto the field at Santiago Bernabéu Stadium in Madrid on Sunday morning (for us).

They should win, but they're going to have to put together a complete game that doesn't include a bunch of self-inflicted wounds to do it.

#2803. Monday Night Football: Zach Charbonnet, Jadarian Price are active for Seahawks vs. Bears

The Seahawks will officially have running backs Zach Charbonnet and Jadarian Price for Monday Night Football.

Charbonnet will make his 2026 debut after returning from a torn ACL that ended his season in the divisional round of the playoffs in January. He set career highs in 2025 with 730 rushing yards and a team-high 12 touchdowns.

The Seahawks activated Price from injured reserve on Monday, and he is active for the first time since Week 3, when he injured his chest. He has 32 touches for 140 yards and two fumbles.

Offensive guard Quenton Nelson will make his Seattle debut after a trade from Indianapolis this week. He will play left guard with Grey Zabel moving to right guard.

The Seahawks won’t have outside linebacker Uchenna Nwosu, who was doubtful with an ankle injury, or back up quarterback Drew Lock, who was questionable with a right elbow injury. Jalen Milroe will backup Sam Darnold, with Lock designated as the emergency third quarterback.

Wide receiver Rashid Shaheed (knee), offensive tackle Charles Cross (ankle), running back Velus Jones Jr. (hamstring), nose tackle Brandon Pili (back) and offensive guard Beau Stephens are the team’s other inactives.

The Bears won’t have cornerback Malik Muhammad (toe). He was limited in practice and given a questionable injury designation.

The Bears’ other inactives are running back Kyle Monangai (thumb/toe), quarterback Case Keenum, defensive tackle Jayden Loving, offensive lineman Jordan McFadden and defensive tackle Jordan van den Berg.

#2802. Warriors on a roll, get fifth straight win behind Steph Curry's 34-point night

SAN FRANCISCO ― The Golden State Warriors have rattled off five consecutive wins following their hard-fought 116-113 win against the Phoenix Suns on Monday, Nov. 2 at Chase Center.

Warriors guard Stephen Curry led the way with 34 points and seven assists, while backcourt running mate Brandin Podziemski dropped 20 points on 50% shooting in the game.

There was no love lost between these two teams, which saw each other on Oct. 24 when the Suns got the upper hand. In the rematch, it was the Warriors who came with energy and fight.

Warriors forward Draymond Green was ejected for the first time this season following a foul against Suns center Khaman Maluach when afterward Green committed a takedown on Maluach. Suns guard Dillon Brooks confronted Green about the push and the two began a shoving match before receiving double technical fouls.

Golden State trailed by 11 points at the time of the technical foul and proceeded to go on a run to fight back into the game before taking over, putting up 42 points in the third period to tie the game 93-93.

The Warriors received a major boost from Charles Bassey, who was effective off the bench scoring 17 points and pulling down 8 rebounds in 23 minutes. Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg had 16 points on 3-of-10 shooting, grabbing five rebounds and five assists.

"Bass was great. What a big night for him," Kerr said after the game. "The rebounding, the scoring. You know he and Yaxel have a really good connection. The pick-and-roll game. He gives him a lot of threat. Yaxel sees the floor so well and he's so big that he finds him when he's behind the defense."

Golden State will host the Miami Heat on Thursday, Nov. 5, beginning at 7 p.m. PT (10 p.m. ET).

#2801. Man City Defender Opens Door to January Exit Amid Manchester United Interest

Is there any hope for Rayan Ait-Nouri at Manchester City?

Ait-Nouri has endured a challenging spell at the Etihad Stadium since arriving from Wolverhampton Wanderers in the summer of 2025, arriving at the club in a deal believed to be worth an initial £31.8 million.

Despite putting in some promising opening performances under Pep Guardiola at the FIFA Club World Cup in the United States and in the opening stages of the 2025/26 season, Ait-Nouri was largely displaced by the emergence of Nico O'Reilly at left-back.

The Englishman would go on to put in a breakthrough campaign in the role, becoming a mainstay in Thomas Tuchel's England international set-up in the process, and winning the PFA's Young Player of the Season come the end of the 2025/26 schedule.

With Enzo Maresca at the helm, some Manchester City supporters felt as though it may be the opportunity for a fresh start for Rayan Ait-Nouri, but the 25-year-old has continued to struggle for game time - with Josko Gvardiol and O'Reilly ahead of him in the left-back pecking order.

That clear lack of opportunities has as such raised question marks over whether there is even a future for the North African star at the Etihad Stadium, particularly after he sought out a possible exit during the final days and weeks of the summer transfer window.

Now, according to Sky Germany's Florian Plettenberg, Rayan Aït-Nouri is 'one to watch' ahead of the upcoming winter transfer window, with the left-back said to be 'open' to leaving Manchester City.

From within the Premier League, it is revealed that cross-town rivals Manchester United 'would like to sign him', in a potential transfer that would not see personal terms emerge as an issue.

However, unsurprisingly, it is pointed out that the 'main issue' in any transfer for the Algerian defender would be the relationship between the two Manchester clubs.

Interest in a potential exit from Manchester City has largely been sparked by a significant drop-off in involvement at the club this season for Rayan Ait-Nouri, often missing out on matchday squads entirely and making just four appearances.

Registering outings in the Carabao Cup's third and fourth rounds as well as two UEFA Champions League matches, Ait-Nouri has however managed to record three assists for Enzo Maresca's side - operating in left-back and a left-winger role.

However, the most concerning is that the Algerian has managed just one start coming in a 5-0 win over Norwich City in the League Cup, appearing as a substitute against Brighton & Hove Albion in the following round, as well as FC Porto and AEK Athens in the Champions League.
